Code: python

        "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters." \
        + " Returns tuple (part, remider)."
        div = 0
        for c in s:

                break

                break
            else:
                div += 1

        return (s[:div], s[div:])
Inputs: 2.2.0~rc5, '2.2.0~rc5', False, 0, '0', ('.', '+', '~'), '2.2.0~rc5', ('.', '+', '~', '-', ':'), '2.2.0~rc5'

Reason in a direct trace-first way.

A productive pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Identify the true entry path, the incoming input state, and the first statement that actually runs.
- Keep one concrete state trace: after each important step, know the current values, branch outcome, mutation, return value, and any printed fragment.
- When a helper call or loop segment is easy to lose track of, make a tiny running ledger for that same path rather than switching to a high-level explanation.
- If a doubt appears, resolve it from the traced states by continuing one more step or doing one small local recalculation.
- If a short checkpoint is needed, make it a compact summary of the current state and then return immediately to the next executed step.
- Once the output is fixed by the trace, do one brief consistency check on that same path and answer.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- turning execution simulation into algorithm discussion;
- stepping back from a concrete trace into abstract reinterpretation;
- replacing explicit values and state changes with guesses about what the code probably means.

class Version:
    def __init__(self, version):
        self._version = version
        self._key = self._parse_version(version)
    def _parse_version(self, version):
        result = []
        for part in version.split('.'):
            # parse each part
            while part:
                if part[0].isdecimal():
                    # numeric part
                    num, part = self._get_part(part, True)
                    result.append(int(num))
                else:
                    # non-numeric part
                    letter, part = self._get_part(part, False)
                    result.append(letter)
        return tuple(result)
```

def _parse_version(self, version):
    result = []
    for part in version.split('.'):
        # parse each part
        while part:
            if part[0].isdecimal():
                # numeric part
                num, part = self._get_part(part, True)
                result.append(int(num))

                letter, part = self._get_part(part, False)
                result.append(letter)

```

If we call `_get_part('2.2.0~rc5', False)`:
- s = '2.2.0~rc5', decimal = False.
- Loop over characters:
  c='2' isdecimal True, decimal False -> condition `elif not decimal and c.isdecimal(): break` at first char, div=0.
- Return ('', '2.2.0~rc5').
If we call `_get_part('2.2.0~rc5', True)`:
- decimal True, c='2' decimal -> div=1; c='.' not decimal -> break.
- Return ('2', '.2.0~rc5').
If we call `_get_part('0', True)`:
- c='0' decimal -> div=1; end -> return ('0', '').
If we call `_get_part('0', False)`:
- c='0' decimal -> break -> return ('', '0').
If we call `_get_part(('.', '+', '~'), False)`:
- s is a tuple, iterating over characters? `c` would be strings '.', '+', '~'. `c.isdecimal()` exists for strings. decimal=False, c='.' not decimal -> div=1; c='+' not decimal -> div=2; c='~' not decimal -> div=3; end -> return (('.', '+', '~'), ''). But `s[:div]` on tuple returns tuple. So output (('.', '+', '~'), ''). If decimal=True, c='.' not decimal -> break at first char, return ((), ('.', '+', '~')).
If we call `_get_part(('.', '+', '~', '-', ':'), False)`:
- similar, all non-decimal, return (tuple, '').
If we call `_get_part('2.2.0~rc5', False)` as above.

def _parse_version(self, version):
    result = []
    for part in version.split('.'):
        # parse each part
        while part:
            if part[0].isdecimal():
                # numeric part
                num, part = self._get_part(part, True)
                result.append(int(num))
            else:
                # non-numeric part
                letter, part = self._get_part(part, False)
                result.append(letter)
    return tuple(result)
```

class LooseVersion(Version):
    component_re = re.compile(r'(\d+ | [a-z]+ | \.)', re.VERBOSE)
    def parse(self, vstring):
        self.vstring = vstring
        components = [x for x in self.component_re.split(vstring) if x]
        for i, obj in enumerate(components):
            try:
                components[i] = int(obj)
            except ValueError:
                pass
        self.version = components
```

And `_get_part`:
```
def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters." \
    + " Returns tuple (part, remider)."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```
Yes.

for part in version.split('.'):
    while part:
        if part[0].isdecimal():
            num, part = self._get_part(part, True)
            result.append(int(num))
        else:
            letter, part = self._get_part(part, False)
            result.append(letter)
return tuple(result)
```
Version = '2.2.0~rc5'
Split by '.' -> ['2', '2', '0~rc5']
- part='2': part[0].isdecimal() True -> `_get_part('2', True)` returns ('2', ''); result append 2; part='' -> loop ends.
- part='2': same -> `_get_part('2', True)` returns ('2', ''); result append 2.
- part='0~rc5': part[0]='0' decimal -> `_get_part('0~rc5', True)`:
  c='0' decimal -> div=1; c='~' not decimal -> break; return ('0', '~rc5'); result append 0; part='~rc5'.
  part[0]='~' not decimal -> `_get_part('~rc5', False)`:
  c='~' not decimal -> div=1; c='r' not decimal -> div=2; c='c' not decimal -> div=3; c='5' decimal -> break; return ('~rc', '5'); result append '~rc'; part='5'.
  part[0]='5' decimal -> `_get_part('5', True)` returns ('5', ''); result append 5; part=''.
Result: (2, 2, 0, '~rc', 5).

So the calls to `_get_part` are:
1. `_get_part('2', True)`
2. `_get_part('2', True)`
3. `_get_part('0~rc5', True)`
4. `_get_part('~rc5', False)`
5. `_get_part('5', True)`

The inputs line includes `'2.2.0~rc5'` multiple times, `False`, `0`, `'0'`, tuples, etc. Not matching.

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters." \
    + " Returns tuple (part, remider)."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```

class LooseVersion(Version):
    component_re = re.compile(r'(\d+ | [a-z]+ | \.)', re.VERBOSE)
    def parse(self, vstring):
        self.vstring = vstring
        components = [x for x in self.component_re.split(vstring) if x]
        for i, obj in enumerate(components):
            try:
                components[i] = int(obj)
            except ValueError:
                pass
        self.version = components
```

    def __init__(self, version):
        if isinstance(version, Version):
            self._version = version._version
            self._key = version._key
            return
        try:
            self._version = parse(version)
        except (InvalidVersion, TypeError):
            self._version = LegacyVersion(version)
            self._key = self._version._key
            return
        self._key = self._parse_version(self._version)

    def _parse_version(self, version):
        result = []
        for part in version.split('.'):
            # parse each part
            while part:
                if part[0].isdecimal():
                    # numeric part
                    num, part = self._get_part(part, True)
                    result.append(int(num))
                else:
                    # non-numeric part
                    letter, part = self._get_part(part, False)
                    result.append(letter)
        return tuple(result)

```
def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```
This is exactly from `pkg_resources._vendor.packaging.version.Version`. The method is used in `_parse_version` to split version strings. def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0

        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0

        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break

            break
        else:
            div += 1

```

```
def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```
This is exactly from `pkg_resources._vendor.packaging.version.Version`. The method is used in `_parse_version` to split version strings. class Version:
    def __init__(self, version):
        ...
        self._key = self._parse_version(version)
        ...
    def _parse_version(self, version):
        result = []
        for part in version.split('.'):
            # parse each part
            while part:
                if part[0].isdecimal():
                    # numeric part
                    num, part = self._get_part(part, True)
                    result.append(int(num))
                else:
                    # non-numeric part
                    letter, part = self._get_part(part, False)
                    result.append(letter)
        return tuple(result)
```

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):

        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break

            break
        else:

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):

            break

            break
        else:

```

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```

            break

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0

        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break

            break
        else:
            div += 1

```

            # parse each part

```
def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```
This is exactly from `pkg_resources._vendor.packaging.version.Version`. The method is used in `_parse_version` to split version strings. class Version:
    def __init__(self, version):
        ...
        self._key = self._parse_version(version)
        ...
    def _parse_version(self, version):
        result = []
        for part in version.split('.'):
            # parse each part
            while part:
                if part[0].isdecimal():
                    # numeric part
                    num, part = self._get_part(part, True)
                    result.append(int(num))
                else:
                    # non-numeric part
                    letter, part = self._get_part(part, False)
                    result.append(letter)
        return tuple(result)
```

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```

        ...

        result = []

            # parse each part

                if part[0].isdecimal():

                    result.append(int(num))
                else:
                    # non-numeric part

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):

            break

            break
        else:

```

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```

            break

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0

        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break

            break
        else:
            div += 1

```

            # parse each part

def _parse_version(self, version):
    result = []
    for part in version.split('.'):
        # parse each part
        while part:
            if part[0].isdecimal():
                # numeric part
                num, part = self._get_part(part, True)
                result.append(int(num))
            else:
                # non-numeric part
                letter, part = self._get_part(part, False)
                result.append(letter)
    return tuple(result)
```

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```
This is exactly from `pkg_resources._vendor.packaging.version.Version`. The method is used in `_parse_version` to split version strings. def _get_part(self, s, decimal):

            break

        else:

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):

            break

            break
        else:

```

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```

            break

def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0

        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break

            break
        else:
            div += 1

```

            # parse each part

```
def _get_part(self, s, decimal):
    "Strips first part of string containing either non-decimal or decimal characters."
    div = 0
    for c in s:
        if decimal and not c.isdecimal():
            break
        elif not decimal and c.isdecimal():
            break
        else:
            div += 1
    return (s[:div], s[div:])
```
This is exactly from `pkg_resources._vendor.packaging.version.Version`. The method is used in `_parse_version` to split version strings.     def __init__(self, version):
        ...

    def _parse_version(self, version):
        result = []
        for part in version.split('.'):
            # parse each part
            while part:
                if part[0].isdecimal():
                    # numeric part
                    num, part = self._get_part(part, True)
                    result.append(int(num))
                else:
                    # non-numeric part
